(collectively referred to as &#147;the Company&#148;). &nbsp;All intercompany transactions and balances have been eliminated. </p> <p style="LINE-HEIGHT:12pt; MARGIN:0px"><br></p> <p style="LINE-HEIGHT:12pt; MARGIN-TOP:0px; WIDTH:24px; MARGIN-BOTTOM:-2px; FLOAT:left">(c)</p> <p style="LINE-HEIGHT:12pt; TEXT-INDENT:-2px; MARGIN:0px; PADDING-LEFT:24px">Basis of Presentation</p> <p style="LINE-HEIGHT:12pt; MARGIN:0px; CLEAR:left"><br></p> <p style="LINE-HEIGHT:12pt; MARGIN:0px; PADDING-LEFT:24px">Certain amounts for 2009 have been revised to conform to the 2010 presentation pursuant to the Company adoption of FASB Accounting Standards Codification (ASC) 810 which requires noncontrolling interests to be classified as a separate component of net income and stockholders&#146; equity.&nbsp; The adopted ASC 810 guidance is effective for the Company&#146;s fiscal year beginning May 1, 2009.</p> <p style="LINE-HEIGHT:12pt; MARGIN:0px"><br></p> <p style="LINE-HEIGHT:12pt; MARGIN-TOP:0px; WIDTH:24px; MARGIN-BOTTOM:-2px; FLOAT:left">(d)</p> <p style="LINE-HEIGHT:12pt; TEXT-INDENT:-2px; MARGIN:0px; PADDING-LEFT:24px">Use of Estimates in Preparation of Financial Statements</p> <p style="LINE-HEIGHT:12pt; MARGIN:0px; CLEAR:left"><br></p> <p style="LINE-HEIGHT:12pt; MARGIN:0px; PADDING-LEFT:24px">The preparation of financial statements in conformity with United States generally accepted accounting principles (GAAP) requires management to make estimates and assumptions that affect the reported amounts of assets and liabilities and disclosure of contingent assets and liabilities at the date of the financial statements and the reported amounts of revenues and expenses during the reporting period. &nbsp;Actual results could differ from those estimates.</p> <p style="LINE-HEIGHT:12pt; MARGIN:0px"><br></p> <p style="LINE-HEIGHT:12pt; MARGIN-TOP:0px; WIDTH:24px; MARGIN-BOTTOM:-2px; FLOAT:left">(e)</p> <p style="LINE-HEIGHT:12pt; TEXT-INDENT:-2px; MARGIN:0px; PADDING-LEFT:24px">Cash</p> <p style="LINE-HEIGHT:12pt; MARGIN:0px; CLEAR:left"><br></p> <p style="LINE-HEIGHT:12pt; MARGIN:0px; PADDING-LEFT:24px">For purposes of the statements of cash flows, the Company considers all highly liquid investments purchased with an original maturity of three months or less to be cash equivalents. &nbsp;The Company had $471,678 of cash in banks as of April 30, 2010. &nbsp;Of that amount, none exceeds the Federal Deposit Insurance Corporation (FDIC) insured limit of $250,000.</p> <p style="LINE-HEIGHT:12pt; MARGIN:0px"><br></p> <p style="LINE-HEIGHT:12pt; MARGIN-TOP:0px; WIDTH:24px; MARGIN-BOTTOM:-2px; FLOAT:left">(f)</p> <p style="LINE-HEIGHT:12pt; TEXT-INDENT:-2px; MARGIN:0px; PADDING-LEFT:24px">Inventories</p> <p style="LINE-HEIGHT:12pt; MARGIN:0px; CLEAR:left"><br></p> <p style="LINE-HEIGHT:12pt; MARGIN:0px; PADDING-LEFT:24px">Inventories consist of ore and are carried at the lower of cost (first in first out) or market. </p> <p style="LINE-HEIGHT:12pt; MARGIN:0px"><br></p> <p style=MARGIN:0px align=center><br><br></p></div> <div style=WIDTH:624px> <p style="MARGIN:0px; FONT-SIZE:12pt" align=center>49</p> <p style=MARGIN:0px><br></p> <p style=MARGIN:0px><br></p> <hr size=1 noshade style="MARGIN-BOTTOM:9px; PADDING-TOP:9px"> </div> <div style=WIDTH:651px> <p style="PAGE-BREAK-BEFORE:always; MARGIN:0px" align=center>BULLION MONARCH MINING, INC. AND SUBSIDIARY</p> <p style=MARGIN:0px align=center>Notes to the Consolidated Financial Statements</p> <p style=MARGIN:0px align=center>For the years ended April 30, 2010 and 2009</p> <p style=MARGIN:0px><br></p> <p style="MARGIN-TOP:0px; WIDTH:60px; MARGIN-BOTTOM:-2px; FLOAT:left"><b>NOTE 1</b></p> <p style="TEXT-INDENT:-2px; MARGIN:0px"><b><u>ORGANIZATION AND SUMMARY OF SIGNIFICANT ACCOUNTING POLICIES (CONTINUED)</u></b></p> <p style="MARGIN:0px; CLEAR:left"><br></p> <p style="MARGIN-TOP:0px; WIDTH:24px; MARGIN-BOTTOM:-2px; FLOAT:left">(g)</p> <p style="TEXT-INDENT:-2px; MARGIN:0px; PADDING-LEFT:24px">Royalty Revenue</p> <p style="MARGIN:0px; CLEAR:left"><br></p> <p style="LINE-HEIGHT:12pt; MARGIN:0px; PADDING-LEFT:24px">The Company recognizes revenues in accordance ASC 605, which clarifies application of U. S. generally accepted accounting principles to revenue transactions. &nbsp;Revenue is recognized as earned; persuasive evidence of an agreement exists, as precious metals are mined and the risks and rewards have been transferred (and sold on the market), prices are fixed or determinable, and cash collections are reasonably assured. &nbsp;</p> <p style="LINE-HEIGHT:12pt; MARGIN:0px"><br></p> <p style="LINE-HEIGHT:12pt; MARGIN:0px; PADDING-LEFT:24px">Royalty Revenue is recognized as estimated ore deposits are sold by the lessee. &nbsp;Adjustments to estimates are recorded at final settlement with the buyer of the ore. &nbsp;&nbsp;</p> <p style="LINE-HEIGHT:12pt; MARGIN:0px"><br></p> <p style="LINE-HEIGHT:12pt; MARGIN-TOP:0px; WIDTH:24px; MARGIN-BOTTOM:-2px; FLOAT:left">(h)</p> <p style="LINE-HEIGHT:12pt; TEXT-INDENT:-2px; MARGIN:0px; PADDING-LEFT:24px">Royalty Receivables</p> <p style="LINE-HEIGHT:12pt; MARGIN:0px; CLEAR:left"><br></p> <p style="LINE-HEIGHT:12pt; MARGIN:0px; PADDING-LEFT:24px">Royalty receivables include certain amounts due from mining lessees. &nbsp;The Company records the receivable once ore deposits have been mined and sold on the market. &nbsp;In general, initial payment is based upon provisional royalties calculated using ounces of gold and silver shipped. &nbsp;Final royalties are paid when final settlement is made by buyers. &nbsp;The Company believes all amounts are fully collectible and therefore has not recorded an allowance for doubtful accounts. &nbsp;The Company charges off and expenses uncollectible accounts when management estimates no possibility of collecting the related receivable. &nbsp;The Company considers royalty receivables to be past due or delinquent based on contractual payment terms.</p> <p style="LINE-HEIGHT:12pt; MARGIN:0px"><br></p> <p style="LINE-HEIGHT:12pt; MARGIN-TOP:0px; WIDTH:24px; MARGIN-BOTTOM:-2px; FLOAT:left">(i)</p> <p style="LINE-HEIGHT:12pt; TEXT-INDENT:-2px; MARGIN:0px; PADDING-LEFT:24px">Impairment of Patents</p> <p style="LINE-HEIGHT:12pt; MARGIN:0px; CLEAR:left"><br></p> <p style="LINE-HEIGHT:12pt; MARGIN:0px; PADDING-LEFT:24px">The Company reviews long-lived assets, at least annually, to determine if impairment has occurred and whether the economic benefit of the asset (fair value for assets to be used and fair value less disposal costs for assets to be disposed) is expected to be less than the carrying value. &nbsp;Triggering events, which signal further analysis, consist of a significant decrease in the asset&#146;s market value, a substantial change in the use of an asset, a significant physical change in the asset, a significant change in the legal or business climate that could affect the asset, an accumulation of costs significantly in excess of the amount originally expected to acquire or construct the asset, or a history of losses that imply continued losses associated with assets used to generate revenue. &nbsp;No such adjustments were required for the fiscal years ended April 30, 2010 and 2009.</p> <p style="LINE-HEIGHT:12pt; MARGIN:0px"><br></p> <p style="LINE-HEIGHT:12pt; MARGIN-TOP:0px; WIDTH:24px; MARGIN-BOTTOM:-2px; FLOAT:left">(j)</p> <p style="LINE-HEIGHT:12pt; TEXT-INDENT:-2px; MARGIN:0px; PADDING-LEFT:24px">Investments</p> <p style="LINE-HEIGHT:12pt; MARGIN:0px; CLEAR:left"><br></p> <p style="LINE-HEIGHT:12pt; MARGIN:0px; PADDING-LEFT:24px">As of April 30, 2010, the Company owns 1,000,000 common shares of Gold Mountain Exploration and Development Company (Gold Mountain), which approximates a ten-percent (10%) interest. &nbsp;The Company also owns 1,651,000 common shares of Sydney Resource Corporation Company, which is approximately a ten-percent (10%) interest. &nbsp;The Company accounts for these investments as &#147;available-for-sale&#148; and records each of the investments at fair market value with an associated adjustment, if necessary, as an unrealized gain or loss (see Note 10). &nbsp;&nbsp;</p> <p style="LINE-HEIGHT:12pt; MARGIN:0px"><br></p> <p style="LINE-HEIGHT:12pt; AND SUBSIDIARY</p> <p style=MARGIN:0px align=center>Notes to the Consolidated Financial Statements</p> <p style=MARGIN:0px align=center>For the years ended April 30, 2010 and 2009</p> <p style=MARGIN:0px><br></p> <p style="MARGIN-TOP:0px; WIDTH:60px; MARGIN-BOTTOM:-2px; FLOAT:left"><b>NOTE 1</b></p> <p style="TEXT-INDENT:-2px; MARGIN:0px"><b><u>ORGANIZATION AND SUMMARY OF SIGNIFICANT ACCOUNTING POLICIES (CONTINUED)</u></b></p> <p style="LINE-HEIGHT:12pt; MARGIN:0px; CLEAR:left"><br></p> <p style="LINE-HEIGHT:12pt; MARGIN-TOP:0px; WIDTH:24px; MARGIN-BOTTOM:-2px; FLOAT:left">(k)</p> <p style="LINE-HEIGHT:12pt; TEXT-INDENT:-2px; MARGIN:0px; PADDING-LEFT:24px">Property and Equipment and &nbsp;Mining Properties</p> <p style="LINE-HEIGHT:12pt; MARGIN:0px; CLEAR:left"><br></p> <p style="LINE-HEIGHT:12pt; MARGIN:0px; PADDING-LEFT:24px"><i>Property and Equipment</i></p> <p style="LINE-HEIGHT:12pt; MARGIN:0px; PADDING-LEFT:24px">Property and equipment are recorded at cost less accumulated depreciation and amortization. &nbsp;Assets are depreciated using the straight-line method over their estimated useful lives ranging from three to five years and not to exceed 40 years for buildings. </p> <p style="LINE-HEIGHT:12pt; MARGIN:0px"><br></p> <p style="LINE-HEIGHT:12pt; MARGIN:0px; PADDING-LEFT:24px"><i>Royalty Interest in Mineral Properties</i></p> <p style="LINE-HEIGHT:12pt; MARGIN:0px; PADDING-LEFT:24px">Royalty interests in mineral properties include acquired royalty interests in production stage and development stage properties. &nbsp;In accordance with the guidance in ASC 805-20-55-37, the fair value of acquired royalty interests in mineral properties is capitalized as tangible assets.</p> <p style="LINE-HEIGHT:12pt; MARGIN:0px"><br></p> <p style="LINE-HEIGHT:12pt; MARGIN:0px; PADDING-LEFT:24px">Acquisition costs of production stage royalty interests are depleted using the units of production method over the life of the mineral property, which is estimated using proven and probable reserves. &nbsp;Acquisition costs of royalty interests on exploration stage mineral properties, where there are no proven and probable reserves, are not amortized. At such time as the associated exploration stage mineral interests are converted to proven and probable reserves, the cost basis is amortized over the remaining life of the mineral property, using proven and probable reserves. &nbsp;The carrying values of exploration stage mineral interests are evaluated for impairment at such time as information becomes available indicating that the production will not occur in the future. &nbsp;</p> <p style="LINE-HEIGHT:12pt; MARGIN:0px"><br></p> <p style="LINE-HEIGHT:12pt; MARGIN:0px; PADDING-LEFT:24px"><i>Impairment of Long-Lived Assets</i></p> <p style="LINE-HEIGHT:12pt; MARGIN:0px; PADDING-LEFT:24px">The Company reviews long-lived assets, at least annually, to determine if impairment has occurred and whether the economic benefit of the asset (fair value for assets to be used and fair value less disposal costs for assets to be disposed of) is expected to be less than the carrying value. &nbsp;Triggering events, which signal further analysis, consist of a significant decrease in the asset&#146;s market value, a substantial change in the use of an asset, a significant physical change in the asset, a significant change in the legal or business climate that could affect the asset, an accumulation of costs significantly in excess of the amount originally expected to acquire or construct the asset, or a history of losses that imply continued losses associated with assets used to generate revenue. &nbsp;No such adjustments were required for the fiscal years ended April 30, 2010 and 2009.</p> <p style="LINE-HEIGHT:12pt; MARGIN:0px"><br></p> <p style="LINE-HEIGHT:12pt; TEXT-INDENT:24px; MARGIN:0px"><i>Exploration and Development Costs</i></p> <p style="LINE-HEIGHT:12pt; MARGIN:0px; PADDING-LEFT:24px">In general, exploration costs are expensed as incurred. &nbsp;When the Company has determined that a mineral property can be economically developed as a result of establishing proven and probable reserves, the costs incurred to acquire and develop such property are capitalized. &nbsp;Costs incurred to maintain current production or to maintain assets on a standby basis are charged to operations. &nbsp;Costs of abandoned projects are charged to operations upon abandonment.</p> <p style="LINE-HEIGHT:12pt; MARGIN:0px; PADDING-LEFT:24px">&nbsp;&nbsp;</p> <p style="LINE-HEIGHT:12pt; TEXT-INDENT:-2px; MARGIN:0px 0px 0px 5px; PADDING-LEFT:0px" align=left>(l)&nbsp;&nbsp; Fair Values of Financial Instruments</p> <p style="LINE-HEIGHT:12pt; MARGIN:0px; CLEAR:left"><br></p> <p style="LINE-HEIGHT:12pt; MARGIN:0px; PADDING-LEFT:24px">The fair value of the Company&#146;s cash and cash equivalents, accounts receivable, and accounts payable approximates the carrying amount due to the short duration of these accounts. </p> <p style="LINE-HEIGHT:12pt; MARGIN:0px"><br></p> <p style=MARGIN:0px align=center><br><br></p></div> <div style=WIDTH:624px> <p style="MARGIN:0px; FONT-SIZE:12pt" align=center>51</p> <p style=MARGIN:0px><br></p> <p style=MARGIN:0px><br></p> <hr size=1 noshade style="MARGIN-BOTTOM:9px; PADDING-TOP:9px"> </div> <div style=WIDTH:651px> <p style="PAGE-BREAK-BEFORE:always; MARGIN:0px" align=center>BULLION MONARCH MINING, INC. AND SUBSIDIARY</p> <p style=MARGIN:0px align=center>Notes to the Consolidated Financial Statements</p> <p style=MARGIN:0px align=center>For the years ended April 30, 2010 and 2009</p> <p style=MARGIN:0px><br></p> <p style="LINE-HEIGHT:12pt; MARGIN-TOP:0px; WIDTH:60px; MARGIN-BOTTOM:-2px; FLOAT:left"><b>NOTE 1</b></p> <p style="LINE-HEIGHT:12pt; TEXT-INDENT:-2px; MARGIN:0px"><b><u>ORGANIZATION AND SUMMARY OF SIGNIFICANT ACCOUNTING POLICIES (CONTINUED)</u></b></p> <p style="LINE-HEIGHT:12pt; MARGIN:0px; CLEAR:left"><br></p> <p style="LINE-HEIGHT:12pt; MARGIN-TOP:0px; WIDTH:24px; MARGIN-BOTTOM:-2px; FLOAT:left">(m)</p> <p style="LINE-HEIGHT:12pt; TEXT-INDENT:-2px; MARGIN:0px; PADDING-LEFT:24px">Income Taxes</p> <p style="LINE-HEIGHT:12pt; MARGIN:0px; CLEAR:left"><br></p> <p style="LINE-HEIGHT:12pt; MARGIN:0px; PADDING-LEFT:24px">The Company applies the guidance in ASC 740 which requires an asset and liability approach for financial accounting and reporting for income taxes, and the recognition of deferred tax assets and liabilities for the temporary differences between the financial reporting basis and tax basis of the Company&#146;s assets and liabilities at enacted tax rates expected to be in effect when such amounts are realized or settled.&nbsp;</p> <p style="LINE-HEIGHT:12pt; MARGIN:0px"><br></p> <p style="LINE-HEIGHT:12pt; MARGIN:0px; PADDING-LEFT:24px">ASC 740 clarifies the accounting and disclosure for uncertainty in tax positions, as defined. &nbsp;ASC 740 seeks to reduce the diversity in practice associated with certain aspects of the recognition and measurement related to accounting for income taxes. &nbsp;The Company is subject to the provisions of ASC 740 and has analyzed filing positions in all of the federal and state jurisdictions where it is required to file income tax returns, as well as all open tax years in these jurisdictions. &nbsp;The Company believes that its income tax filing positions and deductions will be sustained on audit and does not anticipate any adjustments that will result in a material adverse effect on the Company&#146;s financial condition, results of operations, or cash flow. &nbsp;Therefore, no reserves for uncertain income tax positions have been recorded pursuant to ASC 740. &nbsp;</p> <p style="LINE-HEIGHT:12pt; MARGIN:0px"><br></p> <p style="LINE-HEIGHT:12pt; MARGIN-TOP:0px; WIDTH:24px; MARGIN-BOTTOM:-2px; FLOAT:left">(n)</p> <p style="LINE-HEIGHT:12pt; TEXT-INDENT:-2px; MARGIN:0px; PADDING-LEFT:24px">Mineral Taxes</p> <p style="LINE-HEIGHT:12pt; MARGIN:0px; CLEAR:left"><br></p> <p style="LINE-HEIGHT:12pt; MARGIN:0px; PADDING-LEFT:24px">The Company pays a minerals tax to the state of Nevada. &nbsp;The Company&#146;s policy is to record royalties prior to the deduction for the mineral tax and to record the mineral tax as an expense. &nbsp;During the years ended April 30, 2010 and 2009, the Company included $259,730 and $189,002, respectively, of this mineral tax in gold tax expenses.</p> <p style="LINE-HEIGHT:12pt; MARGIN:0px"><br></p> <p style="LINE-HEIGHT:12pt; MARGIN-TOP:0px; WIDTH:24px; MARGIN-BOTTOM:-2px; FLOAT:left">(o)</p> <p style="LINE-HEIGHT:12pt; TEXT-INDENT:-2px; MARGIN:0px; PADDING-LEFT:24px">Net Income Per Share </p> <p style="LINE-HEIGHT:12pt; MARGIN:0px; CLEAR:left"><br></p> <p style="LINE-HEIGHT:12pt; MARGIN:0px; PADDING-LEFT:24px">The Company computes basic income per share by dividing net income available to common shareholders by the weighted average number of common shares outstanding during the period. &nbsp;Diluted net income per share reflects the dilution of potential common shares outstanding such as stock options and unvested restricted stock during the period using the treasury stock method. &nbsp;Basic and diluted earnings per share are equal because the Company does not have stock options or other common share equivalents.</p> <p style="LINE-HEIGHT:12pt; MARGIN:0px"><br></p> <p style="LINE-HEIGHT:12pt; MARGIN-TOP:0px; WIDTH:24px; MARGIN-BOTTOM:-2px; FLOAT:left">(p)</p> <p style="LINE-HEIGHT:12pt; TEXT-INDENT:-2px; MARGIN:0px; PADDING-LEFT:24px">Impact of New Accounting Standards</p> <p style="LINE-HEIGHT:12pt; MARGIN:0px; CLEAR:left"><br></p> <p style="LINE-HEIGHT:12pt; MARGIN:0px; PADDING-LEFT:24px"><u>SFAS 166 Accounting for Transfers of Financial Assets &#150; an amendment of SFAS 140 (June 2009)</u></p> <p style="LINE-HEIGHT:12pt; MARGIN:0px; PADDING-LEFT:24px">SFAS 166, which has been codified into ASC Topic 860, is intended to improve the relevance, representational faithfulness, and comparability of the information that a reporting entity provides in its financial statements about a transfer of financial assets: the effects of a transfer on its financial position, financial performance , and cash flows: and a transferor&#146;s continuing involvement, if any, in transferred financial assets. This statement must be applied as of the beginning of each reporting entity&#146;s first annual reporting period that begins after November 15, 2009. The Company does not expect the adoption of FAS 166 to have an impact on the Company&#146;s results of operations, financial condition or cash flows.</p> <p style=MARGIN:0px><br></p> <p style=MARGIN:0px align=center><br><br></p></div> <div style=WIDTH:624px> <p style="MARGIN:0px; FONT-SIZE:12pt" align=center>52</p> <p style=MARGIN:0px><br></p> <p style=MARGIN:0px><br></p> <hr size=1 noshade style="MARGIN-BOTTOM:9px; PADDING-TOP:9px"> </div> <div style=WIDTH:651px> <p style="PAGE-BREAK-BEFORE:always; MARGIN:0px" align=center>BULLION MONARCH MINING, INC. AND SUBSIDIARY</p> <p style=MARGIN:0px align=center>Notes to the Consolidated Financial Statements</p> <p style=MARGIN:0px align=center>For the years ended April 30, 2010 and 2009</p> <p style="LINE-HEIGHT:12pt; MARGIN:0px"><br></p> <p style="LINE-HEIGHT:12pt; MARGIN-TOP:0px; WIDTH:60px; MARGIN-BOTTOM:-2px; FLOAT:left"><b>NOTE 1</b></p> <p style="LINE-HEIGHT:12pt; TEXT-INDENT:-2px; MARGIN:0px"><b><u>ORGANIZATION AND SUMMARY OF SIGNIFICANT ACCOUNTING POLICIES (CONTINUED)</u></b></p> <p style="LINE-HEIGHT:12pt; MARGIN:0px; CLEAR:left"><br></p> <p style="LINE-HEIGHT:12pt; MARGIN-TOP:0px; WIDTH:24px; MARGIN-BOTTOM:-2px; FLOAT:left">(p)</p> <p style="LINE-HEIGHT:12pt; TEXT-INDENT:-2px; MARGIN:0px; PADDING-LEFT:24px">Impact of New Accounting Standards (Continued)</p> <p style="LINE-HEIGHT:12pt; MARGIN:0px; CLEAR:left"><br></p> <p style="LINE-HEIGHT:12pt; MARGIN:0px; PADDING-LEFT:24px"><u>SFAS 167 Amendments to FASB Interpretation No. 46(R) (June 2009)</u></p> <p style="LINE-HEIGHT:12pt; MARGIN:0px; PADDING-LEFT:24px">SFAS 167, which has been codified into ASC Topic 810, is intended to (1) address the effects on certain provisions of FASB Interpretation No. 46 (revised December 2003), Consolidation of Variable Interest Entities, as a result of the elimination of the qualifying special-purpose entity concept in SFAS 166, and (2) constituent concerns about the application of certain key provisions of Interpretation 46(R), including those in which the accounting and disclosures under the Interpretation do not always provide timely and useful information about an enterprise&#146;s involvement in a variable interest entity. This statement must be applied as of the beginning of each reporting entity&#146;s first annual reporting period that begins after November 15, 2009. The Company does not expect the adoption of SFAS 167 to have an impact on the Company&#146;s results of operations, financial condition or cash flows.</p> <p style="LINE-HEIGHT:12pt; MARGIN:0px"><br></p> <p style="LINE-HEIGHT:12pt; MARGIN:0px; PADDING-LEFT:24px"><u>SFAS 168 The FASB Accounting Standards Codification and the Hierarchy of Generally Accepted Accounting Principles (June 2009)</u></p> <p style="LINE-HEIGHT:12pt; MARGIN:0px; PADDING-LEFT:24px">FAS 168, which has been codified into ASC Topic 105, will become the source of authoritative U.S. generally accepted accounting principles (GAAP) recognized by the FASB to be applied by nongovernmental entities. Rules and interpretive releases of the SEC under authority of federal securities laws are also sources of authoritative GAAP for SEC registrants. On the effective date of this Statement, the Codification will supersede all then-existing non-SEC accounting and reporting standards. All other nongrandfathered non-SEC accounting literature not included in the Codification will become nonauthoritative. This statement is effective for financial statements issued for interim and annual periods ending after September 15, 2009.The Company does not expect the adoption of SFAS 168 to have an impact on the Company&#146;s results of operations, financial condition or cash flows.</p> <p style="LINE-HEIGHT:12pt; MARGIN:0px"><br></p> <p style="MARGIN:0px; PADDING-LEFT:24px"><u>Accounting Standards Update No. 1010-06: Editorial and maintenance update 2010-06</u> &nbsp;</p> <p style="MARGIN:0px; PADDING-LEFT:24px">In January 2010, the FASB issued authoritative guidance requiring new disclosures and clarifying some existing disclosure requirements about fair value measurement.&nbsp;&nbsp;Under the new guidance, a reporting entity should (a) disclose separately the amounts of significant transfers in and out of Level 1 and Level 2 fair value measurements and describe the reasons for the transfers, and (b) present separately information about purchases, sales, issuances, and settlements in the reconciliation for fair value measurements using significant unobservable inputs.&nbsp;&nbsp;This guidance is effective for interim and annual reporting periods beginning after December 15, 2009, except for the disclosures about purchases, sales, issuances, and settlements in the roll forward of activity in Level 3 fair value measurements.&nbsp;&nbsp;Those disclosures are effective for fiscal years beginning after December 15, 2010, and for interim periods within those fiscal years.&nbsp;&nbsp;The new guidance requires only enhanced disclosures and the Company does not expect this guidance to have a material impact on its consolidated financial statements.</p> <p style="LINE-HEIGHT:12pt; The Company accounts for treasury stock using the cost method. &nbsp;&nbsp;</p> <p style="LINE-HEIGHT:12pt; MARGIN:0px"><br></p> <p style="LINE-HEIGHT:12pt; MARGIN:0px">During February 2010, the Company&#146;s Board of Directors authorized a $1 million share repurchase program. &nbsp;The Company plans to repurchase its shares for cash, from time to time in the open market, through block trades or otherwise, through December 31, 2010, with no mandatory minimum number of shares to be repurchased</p> <p style="LINE-HEIGHT:12pt; MARGIN:0px"><br></p> <p style="LINE-HEIGHT:12pt; MARGIN-TOP:0px; WIDTH:60px; MARGIN-BOTTOM:-2px; FLOAT:left"><b>NOTE 5</b></p> <p style="LINE-HEIGHT:12pt; TEXT-INDENT:-2px; MARGIN:0px"><b><u>NON-CONTROLLING INTEREST/INTERCOMPANY TRANSACTIONS</u></b></p> <p style="LINE-HEIGHT:12pt; MARGIN:0px; CLEAR:left"><br></p> <p style="LINE-HEIGHT:12pt; MARGIN:0px">Bullion originally owned 100% of EnShale Inc.&#146;s common stock. &nbsp;On March 10, 2006, however, the Company agreed to issue shares to the officers of EnShale Inc. in lieu of compensation. &nbsp;The Company issued 8,000,000 shares of EnShale Inc. common stock (equivalent to $264,000 or $0.033 per share) to the officers for compensation. &nbsp;On April 30, 2008, EnShale Inc. issued 20,000,000 shares of its common stock to Bullion for the purchase of oil shale leases. &nbsp;As of April 30, 2010, Bullion owned 80% of EnShale Inc.</p> <p style="LINE-HEIGHT:12pt; MARGIN:0px"><br></p> <p style="LINE-HEIGHT:12pt; MARGIN:0px">As of April 30, 2010, EnShale Inc. owed Bullion $4,019,253 for funds advanced. &nbsp;In addition, EnShale Inc. also owed Bullion $225,130 in accrued interest. &nbsp;Bullion loaned EnShale Inc. the cash so EnShale Inc. could pay its operating expenses and construction of demonstration and testing plant. &nbsp;Both the short-term loan and accrued interest were eliminated in these consolidated financial statements.</p> <p style="LINE-HEIGHT:12pt; MARGIN:0px" align=center>BULLION MONARCH MINING, INC. AND SUBSIDIARY</p> <p style=MARGIN:0px align=center>Notes to the Consolidated Financial Statements</p> <p style=MARGIN:0px align=center>For the years ended April 30, 2010 and 2009</p> <p style="LINE-HEIGHT:12pt; MARGIN:0px"><br></p> <p style="LINE-HEIGHT:12pt; MARGIN-TOP:0px; WIDTH:60px; MARGIN-BOTTOM:-2px; FLOAT:left"><b>NOTE 6</b></p> <p style="LINE-HEIGHT:12pt; TEXT-INDENT:-2px; MARGIN:0px"><b><u>MINING PROPERTIES AND MINERAL LEASES</u></b></p> <p style="LINE-HEIGHT:12pt; MARGIN:0px; CLEAR:left"><br></p> <p style="LINE-HEIGHT:12pt; MARGIN:0px"><b><i>Royalty Properties</i></b></p> <p style="LINE-HEIGHT:12pt; MARGIN:0px"><br></p> <p style="LINE-HEIGHT:12pt; MARGIN:0px"><b>Newmont Bullion Royalty Claim Block</b></p> <p style="LINE-HEIGHT:12pt; MARGIN:0px"><br></p> <p style="LINE-HEIGHT:12pt; MARGIN:0px"><b>&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp; Leeville Mine:</b> &nbsp;This property is located in Eureka County, Nevada, in the Carlin Trend, sections 1, 2, 10, 11, and 12 of Township 35 North, Range 50 East and is accessed via improved paved roads. &nbsp;The property is currently being mined and Bullion is receiving a continuing one-percent (1%) gross smelter return royalty. &nbsp;We have claimed that this 1% royalty as defined in the May 10, 1979, Leeville/East Ore Mine Agreement also applies to an area of interest.&nbsp;&nbsp;Because of statements attributed to the Leeville Mine manager in an article in the Mining Quarterly Winter 2009-2010 issue, Bullion management believes the Leeville Mine, which accounts for the vast majority of Bullion&#146;s royalty revenue, has a mine life of at least 10 plus years at full production. The Leeville mine reached full production in August 2008 and royalties from this period were included in revenue for the year ended April 30, 2009, which totaled $3,799,890. The fiscal year ended April 30, 2010, was the first complete year in which the Leeville mine operated at full production, which contributed most of Bullion&#146;s total royalty revenue of $5,214,603.&nbsp; The properties are carried on the consolidated balance sheets of the Company at April 30, 2010 and 2009, at their historical cost of $360. &nbsp;</p> <p style="LINE-HEIGHT:12pt; MARGIN:0px"><br></p> <p style="LINE-HEIGHT:12pt; MARGIN:0px"><b>&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp; East Ore Body Mine:</b> This property is located in Eureka County, Nevada, in the Carlin Trend, sections 1, 2, 10, 11, and 12 of Township 35 North, Range 50 East and is accessed via improved paved roads. &nbsp;The property is currently being mined and Bullion is receiving a continuing one-percent (1%) gross smelter return royalty. &nbsp;We have claimed that this 1% royalty as defined in the May 10, 1979, Leeville/East Ore Mine Agreement also applies to an area of interest. &nbsp;The East Ore Body Mine continues to produce small amounts of gold attributable to our royalty. &nbsp;During the past year royalties to Bullion from this mine totaled approximately $48,000. Mine life and future production are unavailable from Newmont at this time. &nbsp;</p> <p style="LINE-HEIGHT:12pt; MARGIN:0px"><br></p> <p style="LINE-HEIGHT:12pt; MARGIN:0px"><b>North Pipeline:</b> &nbsp;This property is located in Lander County, Nevada, Township 29 North, Range 47 East and is accessed via improved paved roads. &nbsp;This property was purchased on June 6, 1979 Currently, an unaffiliated mining company, Nevada Rae Gold, has been given rights to mine gold from the placer deposits (the top gravel layer) on this property. &nbsp;We have an advanced guaranteed royalty of $22,500 minimum per year. &nbsp;The production royalty is $0.50 per yard of ore processed or 4% of net profit, whichever is greater. &nbsp;No estimates have been made on the mineral deposits on the land</p> <p style="LINE-HEIGHT:12pt; MARGIN:0px"><br></p> <p style="LINE-HEIGHT:12pt; MARGIN:0px"><b>Maggie Creek:</b> &nbsp;This property is located in Eureka County, Nevada, seven (7) miles north of Carlin City, Nevada, and is accessed via improved paved roads. &nbsp;Bullion&#146;s interest in this property is future three-percent (3%) royalty income from operations performed by others mining this property. &nbsp;No operations have commenced on this property. &nbsp;Reserve estimates on this property, if any, are not available to us. &nbsp;The value of this property was derived from the exploration and development costs as described in Note 1(k) as well as the par value of stock issued to another company in exchange for their assets, of which the Maggie Creek property (jointly owned by Bullion and the other company) was the primary asset.</p> <p style="LINE-HEIGHT:12pt; This property is accessible by air or river transportation. &nbsp;During the year ended April 30, 2010, the Company agreed to a Letter of Intent that outlined the terms of participation in a joint venture to develop the Bom Jardim and Bom Jesus mining properties. &nbsp;The properties were held in a subsidiary of Dourave Mining and Exploration Inc. (Dourave). &nbsp;The Company forwarded monies to Dourave during the year ended April 30, 2010, with the expectation to obtain a future 33.3% capital interest in the legal entity that was to be organized to own the properties. &nbsp;Monies were also forwarded to pay for certain exploration costs. &nbsp;&nbsp;</p> <p style=MARGIN:0px><br></p> <p style=MARGIN:0px>During June 2010, the long process of forming the required Brazilian legal entity and getting it registered with the Brazilian government was finally completed with the registration of Dourave-Bullion Mineracao E Exploracao Mineral Ltda (DBM),&nbsp; DBM is the Brazilian entity that will own and manage the Bom Jesus and Bom Jardim mining properties and will perform the exploration.&nbsp; Also in June 2010, the Company executed the legal documents to formally organize Dourave-Bullion Limited Partnership (a Utah limited partnership), the joint venture entity that will own DBM.&nbsp;&nbsp; The Dourave subsidiary is now in the process of formally contributing the Bom Jardim and Bom Jesus mining properties to DBM.&nbsp; Bullion&#146;s 33.3% interest in Dourave-Bullion Limited Partnership was formally obtained and valued at $167,765, which amount represents the final valuation for Bullion&#146;s 33.3% interest in the Bom Jardim and Bom Jesus mining properties. &nbsp;<font style=FONT-SIZE:12pt>&nbsp;</font>The Company has the right to convert its 33.3% interest in the Dourave-Bullion Limited Partnership for a 4.5% net smelter return royalty from the production of the mining properties.</p> <p style=MARGIN:0px><br></p> <p style=MARGIN:0px>In addition to the $167,765 capital contribution commitment, the Company agreed to fund certain exploration activities of DBM and has forwarded $1,425,470 as of April 30, 2010. &nbsp;The Company&#146;s total unpaid exploration funding commitment at April 30, 2010, totaled $406,765, which is expected to be paid-in-full by August 2010. &nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;</p> <p style=MARGIN:0px><br></p> <p style=MARGIN:0px>The Company accounts for this investment utilizing the equity<a name=_GoBack> method. &nbsp;For the year ended April 30, 2010, the Company&#146;s capital contribution of $167,765 has been recorded in the consolidated statement of income as a loss from joint venture. The Company&#146;s exploration funding of $1,425,470 has also been recorded in the consolidated statement of income as a loss from joint venture.</p> <p style=MARGIN:0px><br></p> <p style=MARGIN:0px>DBM&#146;s management decided to focus the majority of its exploration efforts on the Bom Jesus property. &nbsp;Of the $1,593,235 expended through April 30, 2010, approximately $1.1 million was used to explore Bom Jesus. &nbsp;DBM has completed its initial phase of exploration on the Bom Jesus property which included: geochemical and geophysical exploration and a 2212.05 meter diamond drilling program.&nbsp; DBM&#146;s focus was to determine the source of coincident geochemical, geophysical and geological anomalies and the potential for an economic gold and polymetallic resource. <font style=FONT-SIZE:12pt>&nbsp;</font>DBM is now awaiting results from the testing of core samples taken from the mining claims referenced above. &nbsp;&nbsp;Upon analysis of the results, DBM will determine the next steps to be taken on the Bom Jesus Property. &nbsp;</p> <p style=MARGIN:0px><br></p> <p style=MARGIN:0px><br></p> <p style=MARGIN:0px align=center><br><br></p></div> <div style=WIDTH:624px> <p style="MARGIN:0px; FONT-SIZE:12pt" align=center>58</p> <p style=MARGIN:0px><br></p> <p style=MARGIN:0px><br></p> <hr size=1 noshade style="MARGIN-BOTTOM:9px; PADDING-TOP:9px"> </div> <div style=WIDTH:651px> <p style="PAGE-BREAK-BEFORE:always; MARGIN:0px" align=center>BULLION MONARCH MINING, INC. WIDTH:60px; MARGIN-BOTTOM:-2px; FLOAT:left"><b>NOTE 7</b></p> <p style="TEXT-INDENT:-2px; MARGIN:0px"><b><u>ENVIRONMENTAL REMEDIATION OBLIGATIONS AND CONTINGENCIES</u></b></p> <p style="LINE-HEIGHT:12pt; MARGIN:0px; CLEAR:left"><br></p> <p style="LINE-HEIGHT:12pt; MARGIN:0px">Because of the nature of the Company&#146;s operations, there is the potential that the Company may be held liable for environmental remediation particularly in regards to the mineral leases acquired during the year ended April 30, 2006. &nbsp;In those lease arrangements, the Company is required to remove all improvements, equipment, etc. within six (6) months following the termination of the lease. &nbsp;The property must also be restored and reclaimed according to applicable laws. &nbsp;Because operations have not commenced on these and other properties where such an obligation exists, no accrual has been made for these contingencies; such an estimate for accrual cannot be made. &nbsp;The Company will continue to evaluate the status of their operations and appropriately accrue liabilities.</p> <p style=MARGIN:0px><br></p> <p style="MARGIN-TOP:0px; The patent is amortized using the straight-line method over its estimated useful life of 16 years. &nbsp;Amortization expense was $31,247 and $31,252 for 2010 and 2009, respectively. &nbsp;At April 30, 2010 and 2009, accumulated amortization totaled $132,812 and $101,565, respectively</p> <p style="LINE-HEIGHT:12pt; AND SUBSIDIARY</p> <p style=MARGIN:0px align=center>Notes to the Consolidated Financial Statements</p> <p style=MARGIN:0px align=center>For the years ended April 30, 2010 and 2009</p> <p style=MARGIN:0px><br></p> <p style="MARGIN-TOP:0px; WIDTH:60px; MARGIN-BOTTOM:-2px; FLOAT:left"><b>NOTE 10</b></p> <p style="TEXT-INDENT:-2px; MARGIN:0px"><b><u>INVESTMENTS (CONTINUED)</u></b></p> <p style="LINE-HEIGHT:12pt; MARGIN:0px; CLEAR:left"><br></p> <p style="LINE-HEIGHT:12pt; MARGIN:0px">ASC 820-10-20 defines fair value as the price that could be received upon the sale of an asset or paid to transfer a liability in an orderly transaction between market participants at the measurement date.&nbsp; ASC 820-10-35 establishes a fair value hierarchy used to prioritize the quality and reliability of the information used to determine fair values.&nbsp; </p> <p style="LINE-HEIGHT:12pt; MARGIN:0px"><br></p> <p style="LINE-HEIGHT:12pt; MARGIN:0px">Categorization within the fair value hierarchy is based on the lowest level of input that is significant to the fair value measurement.&nbsp; The fair value hierarchy is defined into the following three categories:</p> <p style="LINE-HEIGHT:12pt; MARGIN:0px">&nbsp;</p> <p style="LINE-HEIGHT:12pt; MARGIN:0px">Level 1: Quoted market prices in active markets for identical assets or liabilities.</p> <p style="LINE-HEIGHT:12pt; MARGIN:0px">Level 2: Observable market based inputs or unobservable inputs that are corroborated by market data.</p> <p style="LINE-HEIGHT:12pt; MARGIN:0px">Level 3: Unobservable inputs that are not corroborated by market data.</p> <p style="LINE-HEIGHT:12pt; MARGIN:0px" align=center>&nbsp;</p> <p style=MARGIN:0px>The following table provides our financial assets and liabilities carried at fair value measured on a recurring basis as of April 30, 2010:</p> <p style="LINE-HEIGHT:12pt; MARGIN:0px">&nbsp;</p> <table style="MARGIN-TOP:0px; FONT-SIZE:10pt" cellpadding=0 cellspacing=0> <tr height=0 style=FONT-SIZE:0px> <td width=203> <td width=13> <td width=95> <td width=13> <td width=88> <td width=13> <td width=84> <td width=13> <td width=96> <td width=5> <tr> <td width=203 style=MARGIN-TOP:0px valign=bottom> <p style="MARGIN:0px; WIDTH:60px; MARGIN-BOTTOM:-2px; FLOAT:left"><b>NOTE 11</b></p> <p style="TEXT-INDENT:-2px; MARGIN:0px"><b><u>COMMON SHARES SUBJECT TO CANCELATION</u></b></p> <p style="MARGIN:0px; CLEAR:left"><br></p> <p style=MARGIN:0px>As part of the reorganization from Old Bullion to New Bullion, as mentioned in Note 1(a), the court ordered that in the event an Old Bullion common shareholders does not vote for or against the Reorganization and Exchange Proposal and do not exchange their respective rights in Old Bullion relating to the Reorganization and Exchange Offer within five years from the date of the approval of thereof in the Fairness Hearing of September 27, 2006, such Old Bullion shareholders&#146; rights and New Bullion shares otherwise issued in such exchange shall be cancelled.</p> <p style=MARGIN:0px><br></p> <p style=MARGIN:0px>The total common shares subject to the cancellation provision at April 30, 2010 and 2009, totaled 5,932,878 and 6,115,954, respectively. </p> <p style=MARGIN:0px><br></p> <p style=MARGIN:0px><br></p> <p style=MARGIN:0px><br></p> <p style=MARGIN:0px><br></p> <p style=MARGIN:0px align=center><br><br></p></div> <div style=WIDTH:624px> <p style="MARGIN:0px; FONT-SIZE:12pt" align=center>63</p> <p style=MARGIN:0px><br></p> <p style=MARGIN:0px><br></p> <hr size=1 noshade style="MARGIN-BOTTOM:9px; PADDING-TOP:9px"> </div> <div style=WIDTH:651px> <p style="PAGE-BREAK-BEFORE:always; MARGIN:0px" align=center>BULLION MONARCH MINING, INC. AND SUBSIDIARY</p> <p style=MARGIN:0px align=center>Notes to the Consolidated Financial Statements</p> <p style=MARGIN:0px align=center>For the years ended April 30, 2010 and 2009</p> <p style=MARGIN:0px><br></p> <p style="MARGIN-TOP:0px; WIDTH:60px; MARGIN-BOTTOM:-2px; FLOAT:left"><b>NOTE 12</b></p> <p style="TEXT-INDENT:-2px; MARGIN:0px"><b><u>PENDING LITIGATION</u></b></p> <p style="MARGIN:0px; CLEAR:left"><br></p> <p style=MARGIN:0px>Bullion asserts that pursuant to the May 10, 1979, Leeville/East Ore Mine Agreement (the &#147;Agreement&#148;) between Old Bullion and Newmont USA Limited, et. al. (Newmont), additional royalties are owed to Bullion. In the Agreement, Old Bullion ceased mining and exploration activities in the Lynn Mining District in Northern Eureka County in Nevada, USA, and in a defined multiple square mile area of interest surrounding that area. In return, Newmont, as claimed by Bullion, agreed to pay Old Bullion a one percent royalty on any production in the Lynn Mining District and the area of interest that was described in the Agreement. &nbsp;Newmont has not paid Bullion the one percent royalty and, therefore, Bullion has filed a legal claim against Newmont. &nbsp;During June 2009, Bullion also filed a legal claim against Barrick Goldstrike Mining, et. al. for unpaid royalties in the Lynn Mining District area of interest. &nbsp;The cases have not yet gone to trial and no adjustments have been made to the financial statements to account for these contingencies. &nbsp;</p> <p style=MARGIN:0px><br></p> <p style="LINE-HEIGHT:12pt; MARGIN-TOP:0px; WIDTH:60px; MARGIN-BOTTOM:-2px; FLOAT:left"><b>NOTE 13</b></p> <p style="LINE-HEIGHT:12pt; TEXT-INDENT:-2px; MARGIN:0px"><b><u>NOTE RECEIVABLE</u></b></p> <p style="LINE-HEIGHT:12pt; MARGIN:0px; CLEAR:left"><br></p> <p style="LINE-HEIGHT:12pt; MARGIN:0px">The Company issued a $100,000 note receivable to&nbsp;an individual&nbsp;on October 5, 2009. The terms of the note are a 4% annual interest rate, compounding monthly according to the exact days in the month, beginning November 1, 2009. The due date on the note is November 1, 2011. No payments are required until the note is due, at which time the note will be paid in full. Interest accrued as of April 30, 2010, totaled $1,967. &nbsp;The note is collateralized by mining claims.</p> <p style="LINE-HEIGHT:12pt; MARGIN:0px"><br></p> <p style="LINE-HEIGHT:12pt; MARGIN-TOP:0px; WIDTH:60px; MARGIN-BOTTOM:-2px; FLOAT:left"><b>NOTE 14</b></p> <p style="LINE-HEIGHT:12pt; TEXT-INDENT:-2px; MARGIN:0px"><b><u>SUBSEQUENT EVENTS</u></b></p> <p style="LINE-HEIGHT:12pt; MARGIN:0px; CLEAR:left"><br></p> <p style="LINE-HEIGHT:12pt; MARGIN:0px"><i>Bom Jardim and Bom Jesus Properties</i></p> <p style="LINE-HEIGHT:12pt; MARGIN:0px">See Note 6 for an explanation of the establishment of the legal structure, during June 2010, that will hold the Bom Jardim and Bom Jesus Properties</p> <p style="LINE-HEIGHT:12pt; MARGIN:0px"><br></p> <p style="LINE-HEIGHT:12pt; MARGIN:0px"><i>Treasury Stock</i></p> <p style="LINE-HEIGHT:12pt; MARGIN:0px">During May 2010, the Company acquired 10,000 shares of treasury stock for $7,275. &nbsp;This share acquisition is part of a $1,000,000 share repurchase program authorized by the Company&#146;s Board of Directors during February 2010. &nbsp;</p> <p style="LINE-HEIGHT:12pt; MARGIN:0px"><br></p> <p style="LINE-HEIGHT:12pt; MARGIN:0px"><i>Mexico Property Agreement</i></p> <p style="LINE-HEIGHT:12pt; MARGIN:0px">During June 2010, Bullion entered into a binding term sheet relating to certain mining rights located in Chihuahua Mexico. The term sheet outlines that upon Bullion&#146;s receipt of documentation to establish, among other items, that the property is free and clear of all liens and tax assessments and that ownership is fully vested, Bullion will make a $100,000 payment for the right to conduct exploration activities on the property for a period of six months or until November 30, 2010. &nbsp;During June 2010, Bullion made a $50,000 exploration right payment, which is fully refundable if receipt of proper title is not provided. &nbsp;&nbsp;Bullion, at its sole discretion, shall have the right to continue exploration activities by payment of $100,000 at six month intervals, through May 31, 2012, at which time the payment requirements increase for each successive period. The above payments may be applied toward a $5,000,000 purchase price on the property. &nbsp;During the exploration period, the property owners retain the right to seek other exploration and purchase offers.&nbsp; Bullion has a right of first refusal to match any exploration or purchase offers.</p> <p style="LINE-HEIGHT:12pt; MARGIN:0px"><br></p> <p style="MARGIN:0in 0in 0pt"><i><font style="COLOR:black; FONT-SIZE:10pt">Office Lease</font></i></p> <p style="MARGIN:0in 0in 0pt"><font style=FONT-SIZE:10pt>On&nbsp;June 15, 2010, the Company leased approximately&nbsp;2,000 square feet of office space in St. George, Utah, on a one year lease, with&nbsp;an option to extend the lease and with monthly payments of $2,000.&nbsp;These premises will&nbsp;be the&nbsp;new location of its principal executive offices, effective July 2010.</font></p> <p style="LINE-HEIGHT:12pt; MARGIN:0px">None; not applicable.</p> <p style=MARGIN:0px><br></p><a name=_Toc265664388> <p style=MARGIN:0px><b><u>ITEM 9A(T): &nbsp;CONTROLS AND PROCEDURES</u></b></p> <p style=MARGIN:0px align=justify><br></p> <p style=MARGIN:0px align=justify><font style=BACKGROUND-COLOR:#ffffff><b>Evaluation of Disclosure Controls and Procedures</b></font></p> <p style=MARGIN:0px align=justify><br></p> <p style="TEXT-INDENT:48px; MARGIN:0px"><font style=BACKGROUND-COLOR:#ffffff>Our management, with the participation of our Chief Executive Officer and Chief Financial Officer, evaluated the effectiveness of our disclosure controls and procedures as of the end of the period covered by this report. &nbsp;Based on that evaluation, our Chief Executive Officer and Chief Financial Officer concluded that our disclosure controls and procedures, as of the end of the period covered by this report were effective such that the information required to be disclosed by us in reports filed under the Exchange Act is (i)&nbsp;recorded, processed, summarized and reported within the time periods specified in the Securities and Exchange Commission&#146;s rules and forms and (ii)&nbsp;accumulated and communicated to our management, including our Chief Executive Officer and Chief Financial Officer, as appropriate to allow timely decisions regarding disclosure. &nbsp;A controls system cannot provide absolute assurance, however, that the objectives of the controls system are met, and no evaluation of controls can provide absolute assurance that all control issues and instances of fraud, if any, within a company have been detected. </font></p> <p style=MARGIN:0px align=justify><br></p> <p style=MARGIN:0px align=justify><font style=BACKGROUND-COLOR:#ffffff><b>Management&#146;s Annual Report on Internal Control over Financial Reporting</b></font><font style=BACKGROUND-COLOR:#ffffff><i>.</i></font></p> <p style=MARGIN:0px align=justify><br></p> <p style="TEXT-INDENT:48px; MARGIN:0px"><font style=BACKGROUND-COLOR:#ffffff>Our management is responsible for establishing and maintaining adequate internal control over financial reporting (as defined in Rule&nbsp;13a-15(f) under the Exchange Act). Our internal control over financial reporting is a process designed to provide reasonable assurance regarding the reliability of financial reporting and the preparation of financial statements for external purposes of accounting principles generally accepted in the United States. </font></p> <p style=MARGIN:0px><br></p> <p style="TEXT-INDENT:48px; MARGIN:0px"><font style=BACKGROUND-COLOR:#ffffff>Because of our inherent limitations, internal control over financial reporting may not prevent or detect misstatements. Therefore, even those systems determined to be effective can provide only reasonable assurance of achieving their control objectives. </font></p> <p style=MARGIN:0px><br></p> <p style="TEXT-INDENT:48px; MARGIN:0px"><font style=BACKGROUND-COLOR:#ffffff>Our management, with the participation of our Chief Executive Officer and Chief Financial Officer evaluated the effectiveness of our internal control over financial reporting as of April 30, 2010. &nbsp;In making this assessment, our management used the criteria set forth by the Committee of Sponsoring Organizations of the Treadway Commission (&#147;COSO&#148;) in Internal Control&nbsp;- Integrated Framework. &nbsp;Based on this evaluation, our management, with the participation of the President and Secretary, concluded that, as of April 30 2010, our internal control over financial reporting was effective.</font></p> <p style=MARGIN:0px><br></p> <p style="TEXT-INDENT:48px; MARGIN:0px"><font style=BACKGROUND-COLOR:#ffffff>This Annual Report does not include an attestation report of our registered public accounting firm regarding internal controls over financial reporting. Management&#146;s report was not subject to attestation by our registered public accounting firm pursuant to the temporary rules of the Securities and Exchange Commission that permit the Company to provide only management&#146;s report in this Annual Report.</font></p> <p style=MARGIN:0px align=justify><br></p> <p style=MARGIN:0px align=justify><b>Changes in Internal Control over Financial Reporting</b></p> <p style=MARGIN:0px align=justify><br></p> <p style="TEXT-INDENT:48px; MARGIN:0px">There have been no changes in internal control over financial reporting.</p> <p style=MARGIN:0px><br></p><a name=_Toc265664389> <p style=MARGIN:0px><b><u>ITEM 9B: &nbsp;OTHER INFORMATION</u></b></p> <p style=MARGIN:0px align=justify><br></p> <p style="TEXT-INDENT:48px; MARGIN:0px">See the &#147;PREFATORY NOTE&#148; inserted before the commencement of our discussion in Part I of this Annual Report and our 8-K Current Report dated June 3, 2010, which was filed with the Securities and Exchange Commission on June 9, 2010. &nbsp;The former accounting treatment of our reorganization with Old Bullion as a recapitalization was changed to quasi-reorganization accounting treatment that resulted in our restating of our consolidated financial statements and the filing of a 10-KA-2 Annual Report for the fiscal year ended April 30, 2009, and 10-QA-1 Quarterly Reports for the quarters ended July 31, 2009, October 31, 2009, and January 31, 2010, all of which were filed with the Securities and Exchange Commission on June 22, 2010. &nbsp;We will also be filing a Form 10 under Bullion Monarch Mining, Inc, (New), </p> <p style=MARGIN:0px><br><br></p></div> <div style=WIDTH:624px> <p style="MARGIN:0px; The net effect of applying quasi-reorganization accounting to the Company&#146;s consolidated financial statements for periods subsequent to September 27, 2006, was to record the utilization of the $519,757 net operating loss accumulated prior to September 27, 2006, as a direct addition to additional paid-in capital. </p> <p style=MARGIN:0px><br></p><a name=_Toc265664390> <p style=MARGIN:0px align=center><b>PART III</b></p> <p style=MARGIN:0px><br></p><a name=_Toc265664391> <p style=MARGIN:0px><b><u>ITEM 10: &nbsp;DIRECTORS, EXECUTIVE OFFICERS, AND CORPORATE GOVERNANCE</u></b></p> <p style=MARGIN:0px><br></p> <p style=MARGIN:0px align=justify><b>Identification of Directors and Executive Officers</b></p> <p style=MARGIN:0px><br></p> <p style="TEXT-INDENT:48px; Don Morris, 66,</i></b> has served as President of Bullion Monarch Mining, Inc. since its incorporation in 2004. &nbsp;Prior to the establishment of the new company, he was President of Bullion Monarch Company, our predecessor, for many years.<font style=COLOR:#ff0000> &nbsp;</font>Mr. Morris is the force behind Bullion Monarch Mining. &nbsp;He has<font style=COLOR:#ff0000> </font>over 40 years experience in the mineral industry from early exploration and development through production. &nbsp;&nbsp;Mr. Morris is best known for his involvement and development of the famous Carlin Trend in Northeast Nevada as President of MM&S Exploration Company, which was acquired by Bullion Monarch Company in 1969. &nbsp;Mr. Morris has worked in the mining industry throughout the world in various executive and management capacities. &nbsp;He has served on various Boards of Directors, including US Copper Corp., Gold Standard of Nevada, Metals Inc., Elko Ready Mix and Arco Aris Minerals. &nbsp;In the early 1990&#146;s, Mr. Morris was invited as a special envoy to study the mineral industry in Russia, Yugoslavia and Bosnia. &nbsp;Mr. Morris has worked throughout North and South America, Europe and Asia. &nbsp;Mr. Morris received a Bachelor&#146;s of Science degree in geology from Brigham Young University in 1966 and completed graduate studies at Colorado School of Mines and McKay School of Mines in Nevada.</p> <p style=MARGIN:0px><br></p> <p style="TEXT-INDENT:48px; MARGIN:0px"><b><i>Peter F. Passaro, 64, </i></b>graduated from The University of Connecticut with a degree in Political Science in 1968. &nbsp;Soon after graduation, he began work at E&P Foundry, a non-ferrous manufacturer that produced products for the lock, pump, mailing and other industries. &nbsp;The company specialized in prototype castings in bronze and aluminum. In 1975, he was promoted to Vice President of E&P Foundry, responsible for manufacturing products for such companies as </p> <p style=MARGIN:0px><br><br></p></div> <div style=WIDTH:624px> <p style="MARGIN:0px; FONT-SIZE:12pt" align=center>66</p> <p style=MARGIN:0px><br></p> <p style=MARGIN:0px><br></p> <hr size=1 noshade style="MARGIN-BOTTOM:9px; PADDING-TOP:9px"> </div> <div style=WIDTH:651px> <p style="PAGE-BREAK-BEFORE:always; MARGIN:0px">Homelite, Pitney Bowes, Singer, Electrolux, Textron and others. &nbsp;In the late 1970&#146;s, he began purchasing the company from its owner. &nbsp;In 1985, he became President and CEO of E&P Foundry and continued working in that capacity, successfully building the business, until he sold his interest in 1998. &nbsp;He has worked as a consultant in that industry since 1998.</p> <p style=MARGIN:0px><br></p> <p style="TEXT-INDENT:48px; MARGIN:0px"><b><i>James Andrew Morris, 42,</i></b> has served on our Board of Directors since 1992. He graduated from Brigham Young University with a degree in Business Finance in 1993.&nbsp; He was employed by Merrill Lynch before founding a retail golf franchise, Utah Golf Equipment, LLC, in 1993. &nbsp;He was the owner/operator of this golf business until the time of its sale in 1999. &nbsp;Mr. Morris then began employment with Eagle Home Mortgage, in its Elko, Nevada, Branch. &nbsp;He held various positions at this firm and was ultimately appointed Branch Manager. &nbsp;During Mr. Morris&#146; tenure as Branch Manager, the Elko branch became the most profitable branch of all Eagle Home Mortgage Branches from 2006 through 2009. &nbsp;In 2004, he established M&P Development, a real estate development company, where he directed the planning and construction of three successful residential subdivisions in Elko, Nevada.&nbsp; He continues to serve as managing partner of M&P Development. &nbsp;In January 2010, Mr. Morris joined Bullion Monarch on a full-time basis, serving as CEO of EnShale and Chairman of the Board of Bullion Monarch. &nbsp;</p> <p style=MARGIN:0px><br></p> <p style="TEXT-INDENT:48px; MARGIN:0px"><b><i>Larry L. Anderson, 74</i>,</b> holds a Bachelor&#146;s of Science in Mining Engineering and a PhD in Fuels Engineering, with a minor in Metallurgy, both from the University of Utah. Throughout his prestigious career, he has advised the U.S. Department of Energy and served as a lecturer for NATO Advanced Studies Institute on Fuel Science and Conversion. Currently, he works as Professor Emeritus for the Department of Chemical Engineering, University of Utah. Dr. Anderson is responsible for over 100 research reports and reviews concerning liquefaction and gasification of coal and conversion of plastics, municipal solid waste, agricultural and other wastes into synthetic liquids and gases. Dr. Anderson is credited with co-authoring two volumes related to the production of synthetic fuels from coal and various waste materials.</p> <p style=MARGIN:0px><br></p> <p style="TEXT-INDENT:48px; MARGIN:0px"><b><i>Robert Morris III, 38</i>, </b>has a Bachelor&#146;s degree in Business Finance. &nbsp;From 1999 to 2002, Mr. Morris was a Branch Manager for Enterprise Rent-a-Car, where he oversaw all aspects of the local operation, including inventory, customer service personnel and related duties. &nbsp;In 2002, he joined Verilease Finance as its Vice President of &nbsp;Sales. &nbsp;He successfully managed all equipment leasing activities and employees. &nbsp;Mr. Morris has been employed by us as Controller since 2005.</p> <p style=MARGIN:0px><br></p> <p style="TEXT-INDENT:48px; MARGIN:0px"><b><i>Philip Manning, 51,</i></b> has over 20 years of experience in various sectors of the finance industry. He received his Bachelor&#146;s of Science degree in Business from Brigham Young University and earned an MBA in International Management from the American Graduate School of International Management in 1984. &nbsp;His financial management experience is diverse, beginning with employment at companies such as RCA Corporation in their finance department. &nbsp;In 1984, Mr. Manning joined William Wright Associates, Ltd., an investment advisory firm, where he rose from accountant to Portfolio Manager. &nbsp;While employed there, Mr. Manning was instrumental in establishing a fund to provide mezzanine financing to small companies. &nbsp;In this capacity, he served on boards and advisory boards, advising companies in a variety of industries ranging from high tech start-ups, equestrian equipment manufacturing to manufactured home builders. Beginning in 1990, he spent several years with Merrill Lynch, where he assisted corporate clients with asset management and received honors from Merrill Lynch for outstanding achievement. &nbsp;In 1995, Mr. Manning began his own business as a consultant. &nbsp;In this role, he worked for business clients, including Bullion Monarch in the late 1990&#146;s. &nbsp;In 1998, he joined Bright Trading, one of the largest proprietary trading firms in the U.S., where he was ultimately appointed Manager of the Draper, Utah Branch Office. &nbsp;As Branch Manager, he was responsible for the hiring, training and supervising of traders, which he engaged in until 2001. &nbsp;Mr. Manning continued trading for his own account and in 2005, commenced consulting for business clients once again. &nbsp;More recently, his consultation business included Bullion Monarch Mining, among other clients. &nbsp;His work with Bullion was primarily in the Company&#146;s accounting and finance functions. His contributions proved that he would make a valuable addition to the Bullion management team, and led the Company to offer him his current position as CFO.</p> <p style="LINE-HEIGHT:12pt; MARGIN:0px"><br></p> <p style="TEXT-INDENT:48px; MARGIN:0px"><b><i>John DeMara, 42, </i></b>earned a Master&#146;s of Accounting degree from Brigham Young University in 1995. &nbsp;He received his Certified Public Accountant license in 1998. &nbsp;In January, 1996, he joined Deloitte & Touche, LLP: Tax Consulting and Advisory Services, as a tax consultant. &nbsp;He worked with clients in the hospitality, high-tech, bio-tech, construction, energy development, real estate and other industries. &nbsp;He worked full time for Deloitte & Touche until 2002. &nbsp;In 1996,he founded DeMara Investment Properties: Acquisition and Property Management, which he continues to operate </p> <p style=MARGIN:0px><br><br></p></div> <div style=WIDTH:624px> <p style="MARGIN:0px; FONT-SIZE:12pt" align=center>67</p> <p style=MARGIN:0px><br></p> <p style=MARGIN:0px><br></p> <hr size=1 noshade style="MARGIN-BOTTOM:9px; PADDING-TOP:9px"> </div> <div style=WIDTH:651px> <p style="PAGE-BREAK-BEFORE:always; MARGIN:0px">successfully. &nbsp;Since 2002, Mr. DeMara has continued to consult for Deloitte & Touche LLP: Tax Consulting and Advisory Services, in San Francisco, on a part-time basis. &nbsp;He has over 12 years of corporate accounting experience in various industries. In addition to being a CPA, Mr. DeMara is a member of the Society of Petroleum Accountants and has experience serving the energy development industry.</p> <p style=MARGIN:0px><br></p> <p style=MARGIN:0px><b>Significant Employees</b></p> <p style=MARGIN:0px><br></p> <p style="TEXT-INDENT:48px; MARGIN:0px">We have no employees who are not executive officers, but who are expected to make a significant contribution to our business. </p> <p style=MARGIN:0px><br></p> <p style=MARGIN:0px><b>Family Relationships</b></p> <p style=MARGIN:0px><br></p> <p style="TEXT-INDENT:48px; MARGIN:0px">R. MARGIN:0px">We have no parents.</p> <p style=MARGIN:0px align=justify><br></p> <p style=MARGIN:0px><b>Director Independence</b></p> <p style=MARGIN:0px><br></p> <p style="TEXT-INDENT:48px; MARGIN:0px">We are not required to have any independent directors serving on our Board of Directors; however, we believe that John DeMara is an independent director, based upon the following definition of NASDAQ, which is quoted below from Rule 5605(a)(2). &nbsp;Our Board of Directors has adopted this definition of an &#147;independent director,&#148; even though we are not required to have independent directors.</p> <p style="MARGIN-TOP:5px; MARGIN-BOTTOM:5px">&nbsp;&#147;(2) &#145;Independent Director&#146; means a person other than an Executive Officer or employee of the Company or any other individual having a relationship which, in the opinion of the Company&#146;s board of directors, would interfere with the exercise of independent judgment in carrying out the responsibilities of a director. For purposes of this rule, &#145;Family Member&#146; means a person&#146;s spouse, parents, children and siblings, whether by blood, marriage or adoption, or anyone residing in such person&#146;s home. The following persons shall not be considered independent:</p> <p style="MARGIN-TOP:5px; MARGIN-BOTTOM:5px">(A) a director who is, or at any time during the past three years was, employed by the Company;</p> <p style="MARGIN-TOP:5px; MARGIN-BOTTOM:5px">(B) a director who accepted or who has a Family Member who accepted any compensation from the Company in excess of $120,000 during any period of twelve consecutive months within the three years preceding the determination of independence, other than the following:</p> <p style="MARGIN-TOP:5px; MARGIN-BOTTOM:5px">(i) compensation for board or board committee service;</p> <p style="MARGIN-TOP:5px; MARGIN-BOTTOM:5px">(ii) compensation paid to a Family Member who is an employee (other than an Executive Officer) of the Company; or</p> <p style="MARGIN-TOP:5px; MARGIN-BOTTOM:5px">(iii) benefits under a tax-qualified retirement plan, or non-discretionary compensation.</p> <p style="MARGIN-TOP:5px; MARGIN-BOTTOM:5px">Provided, however, that in addition to the requirements contained in this paragraph (B), audit committee members are also subject to additional, more stringent requirements under Rule 5605(c)(2).</p> <p style=MARGIN:5px><br><br></p></div> <div style=WIDTH:624px> <p style="MARGIN:0px; FONT-SIZE:12pt" align=center>73</p> <p style=MARGIN:0px><br></p> <p style=MARGIN:0px><br></p> <hr size=1 noshade style="MARGIN-BOTTOM:9px; PADDING-TOP:9px"> </div> <div style=WIDTH:651px> <p style="PAGE-BREAK-BEFORE:always; MARGIN-TOP:5px; MARGIN-BOTTOM:5px">(C) a director who is a Family Member of an individual who is, or at any time during the past three years was, employed by the company as an Executive Officer;</p> <p style="MARGIN-TOP:5px; MARGIN-BOTTOM:5px">(D) a director who is, or has a Family Member who is, a partner in, or a controlling Shareholder or an Executive Officer of, any organization to which the Company made, or from which the Company received, payments for property or services in the current or any of the past three fiscal years that exceed 5% of the recipient&#146;s consolidated gross revenues for that year, or $200,000, whichever is more, other than the following:</p> <p style="MARGIN-TOP:5px; MARGIN-BOTTOM:5px">(i) payments arising solely from investments in the Company&#146;s securities; or</p> <p style="MARGIN-TOP:5px; MARGIN-BOTTOM:5px">(ii) payments under non-discretionary charitable contribution matching programs.</p> <p style="MARGIN-TOP:5px; MARGIN-BOTTOM:5px">(E) a director of the Company who is, or has a Family Member who is, employed as an Executive Officer of another entity where at any time during the past three years any of the Executive Officers of the Company serve on the compensation committee of such other entity; or</p> <p style="MARGIN-TOP:5px; MARGIN-BOTTOM:5px">(F) a director who is, or has a Family Member who is, a current partner of the Company&#146;s outside auditor, or was a partner or employee of the Company&#146;s outside auditor who worked on the Company&#146;s audit at any time during any of the past three years.</p> <p style="MARGIN-TOP:5px; MARGIN-BOTTOM:5px">(G) in the case of an investment company, in lieu of paragraphs (A)-(F), a director who is an &#147;interested person&#148; of the Company as defined in Section 2(a)(19) of the Investment Company Act of 1940, other than in his or her capacity as a member of the board of directors or any board committee.<a name=nasdaq-rule_IM-5605><a name=chp_1_1_4_2_8_4>&#148;</p><a name=_Toc265664395> <p style=MARGIN:0px>&nbsp;</p> <p style=MARGIN:0px><b><u>ITEM 14: &nbsp;PRINCIPAL ACCOUNTING FEES AND SERVICES</u></b></p> <p style=MARGIN:0px><br></p> <p style="TEXT-INDENT:48px; Additionally, all services rendered by our principal accountant are performed pursuant to a written engagement letter between us and the principal accountant. &nbsp;Our Board of Directors is presently considering adopting an Audit Committee Charter and naming members of an Audit Committee.</p> <p style=MARGIN:0px><br></p><a name=_Toc265664396> <p style=MARGIN:0px align=center><b>PART IV</b></p> <p style=MARGIN:0px align=center><br></p><a name=_Toc265664397> <p style=MARGIN:0px><b><u>ITEM 15: &nbsp;EXHIBITS, FINANCIAL STATEMENT SCHEDULES</u></b></p> <p style=MARGIN:0px><br></p> <p style=MARGIN:0px>(a)(1)(2)&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;Financial Statements. &nbsp;See our audited consolidated financial statements for the fiscal years ended April 30, 2010, and 2009, contained in Part II, Item 8, which are incorporated herein by this reference.</p> <p style=MARGIN:0px><br></p> <p style=MARGIN:0px>(a)(3)&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;Exhibits. &nbsp;The following Exhibits are filed as part of this Annual Report:</p> <p style=MARGIN:0px><br></p> <p style=MARGIN:0px><b><u>No.</u>&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;<u>Description</u></b></p> <p style=MARGIN:0px><br></p> <table style="MARGIN-TOP:0px;
